You have the "Audio" half of the equation down perfectly, but the "Broadcast Video/IT" half is a significant gap.

- **Project Coordination:** "Leading and coordinating small to medium-sized technology projects." -> Resume: "Built the festival’s entire audio operation from the ground up... established all stage changeover procedures, mic plots, and technical workflows."
- **Mentorship:** "Providing mentorship and on-the-job training." -> Resume: "Manage a crew of 5–6 audio technicians and stagehands per event."
- **Documentation:** "Developing and maintaining comprehensive technical documentation." -> Resume: "Process and log annual speaker traces... ensuring consistent performance benchmarks."

### 4. REQUIREMENTS WITH EXPERIENCE BUT NOT HIGHLIGHTED
- **Video Conferencing (Zoom/Teams/WebEx):** You likely use these for the "Pharmaceutical speaker trainings" and "Institutional Investor roundtables" mentioned in your resume. You should explicitly mention integrating these into hardware codecs (Cisco/Poly) or USB interfaces.
- **Root Cause Analysis:** Your experience with SMAART and "tuning phase, alignment, and coverage on-site" is the definition of technical root cause analysis. You should frame this as "Systemic Audio Optimization and Troubleshooting" to align with IT terminology.
- **Customer Service/Faculty Interaction:** You have mixed for a US President and Fortune 500 CEOs. This "high-stakes" communication is the ultimate version of the "expert consultation" they are looking for with faculty.

### 5. TRUE GAPS
- **Enterprise IT Management (Critical):** The post requires SCCM, Jamf, Active Directory, and anti-virus suite management. Your resume shows no experience in enterprise endpoint management.
- **Control System Hardware (Moderate):** They specifically ask for Crestron/Extron and Audio DSPs (Q-SYS/Biamp). While you know live consoles, you haven't listed these fixed-install platforms.
- **Industry Certifications:** They prefer CompTIA A+, Network+, or AVIXA CTS. You have Dante and Accounting, but lack the IT-specific credentials.

### 6. RED FLAGS & LOGISTICS
- **Location & Relocation:** DC is on your approved list.
- **Salary Analysis:**
  - **New Salary (High End):** $87,558
  - **Annual Relocation Premium (DC):** +$4,608/yr (Chicago to DC transition).
  - **Car Costs:** $0 (DC Petworth/Campus is walkable/transit-friendly).
  - **Net Annual Gain:** ($87,558 - $85,000) - $4,608 = **-$2,050 (Loss)**.
  - **Benefit Adjustment:** Adding ~$20k in value for health/401k/PTO makes this a net positive of ~$18k over freelance, but *only* if you land at the very top of their pay scale.
- **Seniority Mismatch:** This is a "mid-level" IT role. You are a high-level live events specialist. You might find the pace of "fixing classroom projectors" and "imaging Macbooks" frustratingly slow and repetitive compared to mixing the WNBA All-Star game.

### 7. VERDICT
1. **Should I apply?** Only if you are specifically looking for a stable, lower-stress "9-to-5" in DC and want to transition away from live "show-site" work into IT. 
2. **Is it worth tailoring my resume?** Yes, significant tailoring is needed to hide your "touring/festival" persona and highlight an "IT/Systems" persona.
3. **What's the single most important thing to change or add?** You must mention any experience you have with **Windows/Mac OS troubleshooting** and **Video Conferencing**, and frame your technical leadership as "Team Management/SOP Development" rather than "Stage Managing."

  <details><summary>Full Evaluation</summary>

- **Type:** In-house permanent role.
- **On-site Requirements:** Highly likely fully on-site or hybrid; requires 24x7x365 shift flexibility (evenings, overnights, weekends).
- **Industry Vertical:** Media & Entertainment / Broadcast Technology.

### 2. MATCH SCORE
**Match Score: 🟠 STRETCH**
While you have the "Live" and "Audio" experience, this is a **Broadcast IT Infrastructure** role, not a production mixing role. You meet the soft skills and general technical aptitude, but you lack the specific Linux and Tier 2 Helpdesk background required for a "Support Engineer" title in a Network Operations Center (NOC).

### 3. REQUIREMENTS ALREADY MET
- **2+ years experience providing technical support:** Meta-requirement met through 8+ years of freelance A1/RF coordination where you "communicate resolution for issues as they arise" and act as a lead technician for 70+ events/year.
- **Experience using ticketing systems:** While not on your resume, your "Shop prep and maintenance" at Technotrix ($3M inventory) involves processing and logging—essentially hardware ticketing.
- **Network Administration (VLAN/Connectivity):** "Dante Level 1 and Level 2 certified" and "Dante, AES67" protocols require understanding of switch configuration and network clocking.
- **Hardware Configuration:** "Assemble and configure hardware interface units" matches your "Shop prep" experience at Technotrix where you "stage, prep, and maintain... digital consoles, amplifiers."

### 4. REQUIREMENTS WITH EXPERIENCE BUT NOT HIGHLIGHTED
- **Customer Rapport:** You have "trusted repeat relationships with 7–8 production companies" and mixed for **President Obama and Fortune 500 CEOs**. This is "high-stakes" customer rapport that far exceeds "phone and email" support. You should emphasize this "VIP/White Glove" support in a summary.
- **Network Troubleshooting:** Your RF coordination (130 channels) and Dante networking require advanced troubleshooting. You should explicitly mention "DHCP vs. Static IP management" and "managed switch configuration" if you've done it for Dante/Riedel networks.

### 5. TRUE GAPS
- **Linux OS Skills:** Your resume shows no Linux/Command Line/Log File experience. This is often a dealbreaker for LTN, as their transport "leaf" nodes usually run on Linux. *Criticality: High/Dealbreaker.*
- **Tier 2 Helpdesk/Zendesk:** You have "Field" experience, not "Call Center/Support Ticket" experience. Transitioning from FOH mixing to a 24/7 NOC desk can be a significant culture shock. *Criticality: Moderate (Learnable).*
- **Shift Work:** You are used to show calls, but "occasional overnights" in a windowless NOC is different from a 3:00 AM festival strike.

### 6. RED FLAGS & LOGISTICS
- **Relocation Analysis (Chicago to DC - Petworth):**
    - **Estimated Salary:** ~$90,000 (Based on DC Technical Support Engineer averages).
    - **Baseline:** $85,000 (Freelance).
    - **Annual Relocation Premium (DC):** +$4,608/yr.
    - **Car ownership:** Not required in DC Petworth (saving you money vs. other cities).

    - **Benefit Factor:** +$20,000 (Health/401k/PTO).
    - **Total True Economic Gain:** ~$20,000.
- **Seniority Mismatch:** You are a Lead A1/RF Coordinator (Senior Level). This role is a "Technical Support Engineer" (Mid-Level). You might find the work repetitive and less creative than mixing live music or coordinating 130ch of RF.

### 7. VERDICT
1. **Should I apply?** Only if you are looking to pivot away from "on-site production" and into "Broadcast IT/Distribution." It is a desk job, not a show-site job.
2. **Is it worth tailoring my resume?** Yes — you must add any instance where you have used Linux/Unix (even if just for Mac Terminal/command line) and emphasize your Dante networking knowledge as "Network Administration."
3. **What's the single most important thing to change or add if tailoring?** Create a "Broadcast IT & Networking" section that moves your Dante/AES67/Switching knowledge to the top, framing it as "managing IP-based media transport."

### 2. MATCH SCORE
**Match Score: 🟠 STRETCH**
The candidate is a highly skilled Live Event Technical Lead (A1/RF), while this role is a Corporate IT Desktop/Infrastructure Lead. While there is overlap in "Executive Presence" and "High-Stakes Environments," the candidate lacks the deep Enterprise IT Administration skills (SCCM, Active Directory, PowerShell) required for a $145k+ banking lead role.

### 3. REQUIREMENTS ALREADY MET
*   **Executive Support Delivery:** Resume lists direct mixing/support for "Ex-President Barack Obama," "Fortune 500 executive suites," and "CEO Fox Sports Network."
*   **High-Profile Meetings:** Resume highlights managing "Institutional Investor roundtables," "WNBA All-Star Game," and "AbbVie National Meetings."
*   **Banking/Finance Experience:** Resume notes work for "JP Morgan Chase," "Wintrust Corp," "First National Bank," and "Baird."
*   **Communication/Professionalism:** Demonstrated by 8+ years of freelance "trusted subcontractor relationships" and "repeat relationships with 7–8 production companies."
*   **Mentorship/Leadership:** Resume mentions "Manage a crew of 5–6 audio technicians" and "mentoring site leads" (equivalent to leading a festival audio crew).
*   **Education:** BS in Accounting from WVU provides a major "culture-fit" advantage in a global banking group.

### 4. REQUIREMENTS WITH EXPERIENCE BUT NOT HIGHLIGHTED
*   **Meeting/Collaboration Tools:** The candidate uses Dante/RF protocols, which are more complex than Webex/Teams. *Suggestion:* Create a "Collaboration Technology" section and explicitly list MS Teams, Webex, and Zoom Room hardware integration.
*   **Project Management:** Building the "Glades Music Festival audio operation from the ground up" is essentially IT infrastructure deployment. *Suggestion:* Frame the festival build as "Infrastructure Design & Implementation."
*   **Ticketing Systems:** While ServiceNow isn't listed, the candidate’s Accounting background implies familiarity with ERP/database systems.

### 5. TRUE GAPS
*   **Enterprise IT Admin (Critical):** No mention of Microsoft SCCM, Active Directory, Group Policy, or Windows Registry. These are "hard" IT requirements for this salary bracket.
*   **Automation/Scripting (Critical):** No PowerShell or automation experience listed.
*   **Virtual Environments (Moderate):** No Citrix, XenDesktop, or VDI experience.
*   **Mobile Device Management (Learnable):** No mention of Intune/Jamf or enterprise iPhone/iPad deployment.
*   **On-Site Requirement:** The candidate prefers "walkable urban areas" (NYC fits), but the 5-day on-site requirement significantly reduces the flexibility they currently enjoy as a freelancer.

### 6. RED FLAGS & LOGISTICS
*   **Financial Analysis (Chicago to NYC):**
    1.  **New Salary:** $170,000 (Midpoint)
    2.  **Relocation Premium (NYC):** +$19,404/yr (Rent $3,100 + Tax 9.5% vs. Chicago 4.95%)
    3.  **Car Ownership:** Not required (Astoria to Midtown).
    4.  **Benefits Value:** +$20,000 (Health, 401k, PTO)
    5.  **Net Annual Gain:** ($170,000 - $85,000) - $19,404 + $20,000 = **+$85,596 net gain.**
    *   *Note:* The financial gain is massive, even with NY costs. It effectively doubles the candidate's take-home pay.
*   **Lifestyle:** NYC (Astoria) is a Walk/Bike score parallel to Ravenswood but at a much higher intensity.
*   **The "Support Technician" Trap:** The job description mentions supporting meetings "along with our dedicated AV support technician." This implies the candidate will be the *Computer/IT guy*, not the *Audio guy*.

### 7. VERDICT
1.  **Should I apply?** Yes, but temper expectations. You are overqualified for the AV portion and underqualified for the IT Systems Admin portion. However, your Accounting degree and Executive experience make you a very "safe" hire for a bank.
2.  **Is it worth tailoring my resume?** Yes — significant tailoring needed. You must de-emphasize "festivals/mixing" and pivot toward "Executive Technical Operations" and "Systems Management."
3.  **What's the single most important thing to change or add if tailoring?** Create a "Technical Systems Environment" section that mirrors their IT stack (Windows 10/11, Networking/Dante, MS Teams) and prominently feature your BS in Accounting at the top to signal "Executive Literacy."
